					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-subjectpageheader">
</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Movement of goods or people between locations</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1320445320">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable"><span>For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/Transport_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Transport (disambiguation)">Transport (disambiguation)</a>.</span> <span>"Transportation" redirects here. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/Transportation_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Transportation (disambiguation)">Transportation (disambiguation)</a>.</span></div>

.navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output print{.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:none!important}}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Transport" <p><b>Transport</b> (in <a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">British English</a>) or <b>transportation</b> (in <a href="/wiki/American_English" title="American English">American English</a>) is the intentional <a href="/wiki/Motion" title="Motion">movement</a> of humans, animals, and <a href="/wiki/Cargo" title="Cargo">goods</a> from one location to another. <a href="/wiki/Mode_of_transport" title="Mode of transport">Modes of transport</a> include <a href="/wiki/Aviation" title="Aviation">air</a>, <a href="/wiki/Land_transport" title="Land transport">land</a> (<a href="/wiki/Rail_transport" title="Rail transport">rail</a> and <a href="/wiki/Road_transport" title="Road transport">road</a>), <a href="/wiki/Ship_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Ship transport">water</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cable_transport" title="Cable transport">cable</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pipeline_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Pipeline transport">pipelines</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Space_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Space transport">space</a>. The field can be divided into <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure" title="Infrastructure">infrastructure</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vehicle" title="Vehicle">vehicles</a>, and operations. Transport enables human <a href="/wiki/Trade" title="Trade">trade</a>, which is essential for the development of <a href="/wiki/Civilization" title="Civilization">civilizations</a>.
</p><p>Transport <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure" title="Infrastructure">infrastructure</a> consists of fixed installations, including <a href="/wiki/Road" title="Road">roads</a>, <a href="/wiki/Railway" class="mw-redirect" title="Railway">railways</a>, <a href="/wiki/Airway_(aviation)" title="Airway (aviation)">airways</a>, <a href="/wiki/Waterway" title="Waterway">waterways</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canal" title="Canal">canals</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pipeline_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Pipeline transport">pipelines</a>, as well as terminals such as <a href="/wiki/Airport" title="Airport">airports</a>, <a href="/wiki/Train_station" title="Train station">railway stations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bus_station" title="Bus station">bus stations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Warehouse" title="Warehouse">warehouses</a>, trucking terminals, refueling depots (including fuel docks and <a href="/wiki/Fuel_station" class="mw-redirect" title="Fuel station">fuel stations</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Seaport" class="mw-redirect" title="Seaport">seaports</a>. Terminals may be used both for the interchange of passengers and cargo and for maintenance.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Means_of_transport" title="Means of transport">Means of transport</a> are any of the different kinds of transport facilities used to carry people or cargo. They may include vehicles, <a href="/wiki/Riding_animal" class="mw-redirect" title="Riding animal">riding animals</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pack_animals" class="mw-redirect" title="Pack animals">pack animals</a>. <a href="/wiki/Vehicle" title="Vehicle">Vehicles</a> may include <a href="/wiki/Wagons" class="mw-redirect" title="Wagons">wagons</a>, <a href="/wiki/Automobiles" class="mw-redirect" title="Automobiles">automobiles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bicycles" class="mw-redirect" title="Bicycles">bicycles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buses" class="mw-redirect" title="Buses">buses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Train" title="Train">trains</a>, <a href="/wiki/Truck" title="Truck">trucks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Helicopter" title="Helicopter">helicopters</a>, <a href="/wiki/Watercraft" title="Watercraft">watercraft</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spacecraft" title="Spacecraft">spacecraft</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Fixed-wing_aircraft" title="Fixed-wing aircraft">aircraft</a>.
</p><p><b>Mobility</b> is concerned with the experience around transportation services, including aspects such as availability, information, usability, accessibility, and affordability (as in <a href="/wiki/Right_to_mobility" title="Right to mobility">right to mobility</a>).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-TRB2000&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TRB2000-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Modes">Modes</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Modes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mode_of_transport" title="Mode of transport">Mode of transport</a></div>
<p>A mode of transport is a solution that makes use of a certain type of vehicle, infrastructure, and operation. The transport of a person or of cargo may involve one mode or several of the modes, with the latter case being called inter-modal or multi-modal transport.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Each mode has its own advantages and disadvantages, and will be chosen on the basis of cost, capability, and route.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Governments regulate the way the vehicles are operated, and the procedures set for this purpose, including financing, legalities, and policies.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In the transport industry, operations and ownership of infrastructure can be <a href="/wiki/State_ownership" title="State ownership">public</a>, <a href="/wiki/Private_property" title="Private property">private</a>, or a <a href="/wiki/Public%E2%80%93private_partnership" title="Public–private partnership">partnership</a> between the two, depending on the country and mode.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Kaminsky&#95;2018&#95;5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kaminsky_2018-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Transport modes can be a mix of the two ownership systems, such as privately owned cars and government-owned <a href="/wiki/Urban_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Urban transport">urban transport</a> in cities.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Many international <a href="/wiki/Airlines" class="mw-redirect" title="Airlines">airlines</a> have a mixed public-private ownership.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Passenger transport may be <a href="/wiki/Public_transport" title="Public transport">public</a>, where operators provide scheduled services, or <a href="/wiki/Private_transport" title="Private transport">private</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Freight transport has become focused on <a href="/wiki/Containerization" title="Containerization">containerization</a>, although <a href="/wiki/Bulk_material_handling" title="Bulk material handling">bulk transport</a> is used for large volumes of durable items.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Transport plays an important part in economic growth and <a href="/wiki/Globalization" title="Globalization">globalization</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but machine-propelled forms cause <a href="/wiki/Air_pollution" title="Air pollution">air pollution</a> and <a href="/wiki/Land_use" title="Land use">use large amounts of land</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> While it<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Vagueness" title="Wikipedia:Vagueness"><span title="What is &#39;it&#39;? Urban transport? (October 2025)">vague</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> is heavily subsidized by governments, good planning of transport is essential to make <a href="/wiki/Traffic_flow" title="Traffic flow">traffic flow</a> and restrain <a href="/wiki/Urban_sprawl" title="Urban sprawl">urban sprawl</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Human-powered">Human-powered</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Human-powered"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human-powered_transport" title="Human-powered transport">Human-powered transport</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG/250px-Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG" decoding="async" width="190" height="253" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG/330px-Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG/500px-Sherpa_carrying_woods.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1944" data-file-height="2592" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Human-powered_transport" title="Human-powered transport">Human-powered transport</a> remains common in developing countries.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Lowe&#95;1993&#95;14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lowe_1993-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pendakur&#95;2011&#95;15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pendakur_2011-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Human-powered transport, a form of <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_transport" title="Sustainable transport">sustainable transport</a>, is the transport of people or goods using <a href="/wiki/Human" title="Human">human</a> muscle-power, in the form of <a href="/wiki/Walking" title="Walking">walking</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pendakur&#95;2011&#95;15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pendakur_2011-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Running" title="Running">running</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Human_swimming" class="mw-redirect" title="Human swimming">swimming</a>. <a href="/wiki/Technology" title="Technology">Technology</a> has allowed <a href="/wiki/Machine" title="Machine">machines</a> to improve the <a href="/wiki/Energy_efficiency_in_transport" title="Energy efficiency in transport">energy efficiency</a> of human mobility on relatively smooth terrain.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Human-powered transport remains popular for reasons of cost-saving, <a href="/wiki/Leisure" title="Leisure">leisure</a>, <a href="/wiki/Physical_exercise" class="mw-redirect" title="Physical exercise">physical exercise</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Environmentalism" title="Environmentalism">environmentalism</a>;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>17<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> it is sometimes the only type available, especially in underdeveloped or inaccessible regions.
</p><p>Although humans are able to walk without infrastructure, the accessibility can be enhanced through the use of roads, <a href="/wiki/Sidewalks" class="mw-redirect" title="Sidewalks">sidewalks</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Shared-use_path" title="Shared-use path">shared-use paths</a>, especially when using the human power with vehicles, such as <a href="/wiki/Bicycle" title="Bicycle">bicycles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Inline_skates" title="Inline skates">inline skates</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Wheelchair" title="Wheelchair">wheelchairs</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>18<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Human-powered vehicles have been developed for difficult environments, such as snow and water, by <a href="/wiki/Watercraft_rowing" class="mw-redirect" title="Watercraft rowing">watercraft rowing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Skiing" title="Skiing">skiing</a>;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>20<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> even the air can be flown through with <a href="/wiki/Human-powered_aircraft" title="Human-powered aircraft">human-powered aircraft</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>21<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Personal_transporter" title="Personal transporter">Personal transporters</a>, a form of hybrid human-electric powered vehicle, have emerged in the 21st century as a form of multi-model urban transport.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>22<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Animal-powered">Animal-powered</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Animal-powered"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Animal-powered_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Animal-powered transport">Animal-powered transport</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Elephant_on_highway_between_Delhi_and_Jaipur.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Elephant_on_highway_between_Delhi_and_Jaipur.jpg/250px-Elephant_on_highway_between_Delhi_and_Jaipur.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Elephant_on_highway_between_Delhi_and_Jaipur.jpg/500px-Elephant_on_highway_between_Delhi_and_Jaipur.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2432" /></a><figcaption>Elephant transporting a person and some cargo on a highway between Delhi and Jaipur, India</figcaption></figure>
<p>Animal-powered transport is the use of <a href="/wiki/Working_animal" title="Working animal">working animals</a> for the movement of people and commodities.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Baggerman&#95;2024&#95;23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baggerman_2024-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Humans may ride some of the animals directly, use them as <a href="/wiki/Pack_animal" title="Pack animal">pack animals</a> for carrying goods, or harness them,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>24<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> alone or in <a href="/wiki/Team" title="Team">teams</a>, to pull <a href="/wiki/Sled" title="Sled">sleds</a> or wheeled <a href="/wiki/Vehicle" title="Vehicle">vehicles</a>. They remain useful in rough terrain that is not readily accessible by automotive-based transportation.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Baggerman&#95;2024&#95;23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baggerman_2024-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Air">Air</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Air"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Aviation" title="Aviation">Aviation</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Airfrance.a318-100.f-guga.arp.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="White jet aircraft coming into land, undercarriage fully extended. Under each wing is a turbofan engine" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Airfrance.a318-100.f-guga.arp.jpg/250px-Airfrance.a318-100.f-guga.arp.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Airfrance.a318-100.f-guga.arp.jpg/500px-Airfrance.a318-100.f-guga.arp.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2360" data-file-height="1535" /></a><figcaption>An <a href="/wiki/Air_France" title="Air France">Air France</a> <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A320_family" title="Airbus A320 family">Airbus A318</a> landing at <a href="/wiki/London_Heathrow_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="London Heathrow Airport">London Heathrow Airport</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>A <a href="/wiki/Fixed-wing_aircraft" title="Fixed-wing aircraft">fixed-wing aircraft</a>, commonly called an <a href="/wiki/Airplane" title="Airplane">airplane</a>, is a heavier-than-air craft where movement of the air in relation to the wings is used to generate lift. The term is used to distinguish this from <a href="/wiki/Rotorcraft" title="Rotorcraft">rotary-wing aircraft</a>, where the movement of the lift surfaces relative to the air generates lift.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>25<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Gyroplane" class="mw-redirect" title="Gyroplane">gyroplane</a> is both fixed-wing and rotary wing.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Fixed-wing aircraft range from small trainers and recreational aircraft to large <a href="/wiki/Airliner" title="Airliner">airliners</a> and military cargo aircraft.
</p><p>Two things necessary for <a href="/wiki/Aircraft" title="Aircraft">aircraft</a> are air flow over the wings for <a href="/wiki/Lift_(soaring)" title="Lift (soaring)">lift</a> and an apparatus for <a href="/wiki/Landing" title="Landing">landing</a>. The majority of aircraft require an <a href="/wiki/Airport" title="Airport">airport</a> with the infrastructure for maintenance, restocking, and refueling and for the loading and unloading of crew, cargo, and passengers.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Many <a href="/wiki/Aerodrome" title="Aerodrome">aerodromes</a> have takeoff and landing restrictions on weight and runway length, and so are not able to handle all types of aircraft.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>27<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> While the vast majority of fixed-wing aircraft land and take off on land, some are capable of take-off and landing on ice, snow,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>28<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and calm water.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Autonomous or remotely-piloted airplanes are known as <a href="/wiki/Unmanned_aerial_vehicle" title="Unmanned aerial vehicle">unmanned aerial vehicles</a>, or UAV. These drones can range in size from less than a metre across to a full-sized airplane.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>30<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> They are capable of carrying a payload, and are being used for package delivery.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>31<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The aircraft is the second fastest method of transport, after the <a href="/wiki/Rocket" title="Rocket">rocket</a>. Commercial jets can reach up to 955 kilometres per hour (593&#160;mph), single-engine aircraft 555 kilometres per hour (345&#160;mph). Aviation is able to quickly transport people and limited amounts of cargo over longer distances, but incurs high costs and energy use; for short distances or in inaccessible places, <a href="/wiki/Helicopter" title="Helicopter">helicopters</a> can be used.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998281&#95;32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998281-32"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>32<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> As of April 28, 2009, <i>The Guardian</i> article notes that "the WHO estimates that up to 500,000 people are on planes at any time."<sup id="cite&#95;ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>33<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>An <a href="/wiki/Aerostat" title="Aerostat">aerostat</a> is a class of lighter-than-air aircraft that gains its lift by containing a volume of gas that has a lower <a href="/wiki/Density_of_air" title="Density of air">density</a> than the surrounding atmosphere. These include <a href="/wiki/Balloon_(aeronautics)" title="Balloon (aeronautics)">balloons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rigid_airship" title="Rigid airship">rigid</a>, <a href="/wiki/Semi-rigid_airship" title="Semi-rigid airship">semi-rigid</a>, or non-rigid <a href="/wiki/Airship" title="Airship">airships</a>; the last is called a <a href="/wiki/Blimp" title="Blimp">blimp</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Lifting_gas" title="Lifting gas">lifting gas</a> is typically helium, as hydrogen is highly flammable. Alternatively, heated air is used in <a href="/wiki/Hot_air_balloon" title="Hot air balloon">hot air balloons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Thermal_airship" title="Thermal airship">thermal airships</a>. Aerostats can transport passengers and a payload over long distances. For example, <a href="/wiki/Zeppelin" title="Zeppelin">zeppelins</a> were used on long-ranged bombing raids during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>34<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Land">Land</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Land"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Land_transport" title="Land transport">Land transport</a></div>
<p>Land transport covers all land-based transport systems that provide for the movement of people, goods, and services. Land transport plays a vital role in linking communities to each other. Land transport is a key factor in <a href="/wiki/Urban_planning" title="Urban planning">urban planning</a>. It consists of two kinds, rail and road.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Rail">Rail</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Rail"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Rail_transport" title="Rail transport">Rail transport</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:ICE1_Schellenberg.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="White electric train with red cheatline emerging from tunnel in the countryside" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/ICE1_Schellenberg.jpg/250px-ICE1_Schellenberg.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/ICE1_Schellenberg.jpg/500px-ICE1_Schellenberg.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="667" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Intercity_Express" title="Intercity Express">Intercity Express</a>, a German <a href="/wiki/High-speed_rail" title="High-speed rail">high-speed</a> passenger train</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Rail_transport" title="Rail transport">Rail transport</a> consists of wheeled vehicles running on tracks, which usually consist of two parallel <a href="/wiki/Steel" title="Steel">steel</a> rails, known as a <a href="/wiki/Railway_track" title="Railway track">railway</a> or railroad. The rails are anchored <a href="/wiki/Perpendicular" title="Perpendicular">perpendicular</a> to <a href="/wiki/Railroad_tie" title="Railroad tie">ties</a> (or sleepers) of timber, concrete, or steel, to maintain a consistent distance apart, or <a href="/wiki/Rail_gauge" class="mw-redirect" title="Rail gauge">gauge</a>. The rails and perpendicular beams are placed on a foundation made of concrete or compressed <a href="/wiki/Soil" title="Soil">earth</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gravel" title="Gravel">gravel</a> in a <a href="/wiki/Track_ballast" title="Track ballast">bed of ballast</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Alternative methods include <a href="/wiki/Monorail" title="Monorail">monorail</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Maglev_(transport)" class="mw-redirect" title="Maglev (transport)">maglev</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hyperloop" title="Hyperloop">hyperloop</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dual_gauge" title="Dual gauge">Dual gauge</a> railways have three or four rails, allowing use by trains with two or three <a href="/wiki/Track_gauge" title="Track gauge">track gauges</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>38<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For <a href="/wiki/Steep_grade_railway" title="Steep grade railway">steep grades</a>, a railway can use an additional toothed <a href="/wiki/Rack_railway" title="Rack railway">rack rail</a> for traction.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>39<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>A train consists of one or more connected vehicles that operate on the rails, known as <a href="/wiki/Rolling_stock" title="Rolling stock">rolling stock</a>. Propulsion is commonly provided by a <a href="/wiki/Locomotive" title="Locomotive">locomotive</a>, which hauls a series of unpowered cars that carry passengers or freight. The locomotive can be powered by <a href="/wiki/Steam_locomotive" title="Steam locomotive">steam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Diesel_locomotive" title="Diesel locomotive">diesel</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Spiryagin&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2014&#95;40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Spiryagin_et_al_2014-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Gas_turbine" class="mw-redirect" title="Gas turbine">gas turbine</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>41<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> or else <a href="/wiki/Electric_locomotive" title="Electric locomotive">electricity</a> supplied by <a href="/wiki/Railway_electrification_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Railway electrification system">trackside systems</a>. Some or all the cars can be powered, known as a <a href="/wiki/Multiple_unit" title="Multiple unit">multiple unit</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Spiryagin&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2014&#95;40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Spiryagin_et_al_2014-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Tram" title="Tram">tram</a> is similar to a train, but is generally smaller, travels shorter distances, and runs on rails that are integrated into the streets. Typically a tram is electric-powered, but they have also been propelled by <a href="/wiki/Horsecar" title="Horsecar">horses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Funicular" title="Funicular">cables</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>42<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Gravity" title="Gravity">gravity</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> or <a href="/wiki/Pneumatics" title="Pneumatics">pneumatics</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>43<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Railed vehicles move with much less friction than rubber tires on paved roads, making trains more <a href="/wiki/Energy_conversion_efficiency" title="Energy conversion efficiency">energy efficient</a>, though not as efficient as ships.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Inter-city_rail" title="Inter-city rail">Intercity</a> trains are long-haul services connecting cities;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998279&#95;45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998279-45"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> modern <a href="/wiki/High-speed_rail" title="High-speed rail">high-speed rail</a> is capable of speeds up to 350&#160;km/h (220&#160;mph), but this requires specially built track. <a href="/wiki/Shanghai_maglev_train" title="Shanghai maglev train">Commercial maglev transport in Shanghai</a> runs at 460&#160;km/h (290&#160;mph).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>46<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Regional_rail" title="Regional rail">Regional</a> and <a href="/wiki/Commuter_rail" title="Commuter rail">commuter</a> trains feed cities from suburbs and surrounding areas, while intra-urban transport is performed by high-capacity <a href="/wiki/Tram" title="Tram">tramways</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rapid_transit" title="Rapid transit">rapid transits</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>47<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> in many cases making up the backbone of a city's public transport.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Freight_trains" class="mw-redirect" title="Freight trains">Freight trains</a> traditionally used <a href="/wiki/Box_car" class="mw-redirect" title="Box car">box cars</a>, requiring manual loading and unloading of the <a href="/wiki/Cargo" title="Cargo">cargo</a>. Since the 1980s, <a href="/wiki/Container_train" class="mw-redirect" title="Container train">container trains</a> have become the dominant solution for general freight,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> while large quantities of bulk are transported by dedicated rolling stock. An example of the latter are specially designed <a href="/wiki/Tank_car" title="Tank car">tank cars</a> for the transport of <a href="/wiki/Hazardous_material" class="mw-redirect" title="Hazardous material">hazardous materials</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>49<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Road">Road</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Road"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Road_transport" title="Road transport">Road transport</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:High_Five.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/High_Five.jpg/250px-High_Five.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="333" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/High_Five.jpg/500px-High_Five.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2667" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Road_transport" title="Road transport">Road transport</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>A road is an identifiable <a href="/wiki/Route_number" title="Route number">route</a>, way, or <a href="/wiki/Trail" title="Trail">path</a> between two or more <a href="/wiki/Location_(geography)" class="mw-redirect" title="Location (geography)">places</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Major&#95;Roads&#95;of&#95;the&#95;United&#95;States&#95;50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Major_Roads_of_the_United_States-50"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>50<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Roads are typically smoothed, <a href="/wiki/Pavement_(material)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pavement (material)">paved</a>, or otherwise prepared to allow easy travel;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-RoadInfrastructureStrategicFrameworkforSouthAfrica&#95;51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RoadInfrastructureStrategicFrameworkforSouthAfrica-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>51<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> though they need not be, and historically many roads were simply recognizable routes without any formal <a href="/wiki/Construction" title="Construction">construction</a> or <a href="/wiki/Maintenance,_repair_and_operations" class="mw-redirect" title="Maintenance, repair and operations">maintenance</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTELay19926–7&#95;52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELay19926–7-52"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>52<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In <a href="/wiki/Urban_area" title="Urban area">urban areas</a>, roads may pass through a <a href="/wiki/City" title="City">city</a> or <a href="/wiki/Village" title="Village">village</a> and be named as <a href="/wiki/Street" title="Street">streets</a>, serving a dual function as urban space <a href="/wiki/Easement" title="Easement">easement</a> and route.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-difference&#95;between&#95;road&#95;and&#95;street&#95;53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-difference_between_road_and_street-53"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>53<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>At least within the U.S., the most common road vehicle is the automobile;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>54<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> a light duty <a href="/wiki/Wheel" title="Wheel">wheeled</a> passenger vehicle that carries its own <a href="/wiki/Car_engine" class="mw-redirect" title="Car engine">motor</a>. Other users of roads include <a href="/wiki/Bus" title="Bus">buses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Truck" title="Truck">trucks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Motorcycles" class="mw-redirect" title="Motorcycles">motorcycles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bicycles" class="mw-redirect" title="Bicycles">bicycles</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pedestrians" class="mw-redirect" title="Pedestrians">pedestrians</a>. As of 2015, there were 950&#160;million passenger cars worldwide, with a projected total of 2.5&#160;billion in 2050.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>55<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Road transport offers road users the flexibility to transfer the vehicle from one lane to the other and from one road to another according to the need and convenience. This combination of changes in location, direction, speed, and timings of travel is not available to other motorized modes of transport.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Rodrigue&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2013&#95;56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rodrigue_et_al_2013-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>56<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> It is possible to provide efficient intracity door-to-door service only by road transport.
</p><p>Some drawbacks are that a road system consumes large amounts of space, are costly to build and maintain (including vehicles), leads to urban congestion, and have only limited ability to achieve <a href="/wiki/Economies_of_scale" title="Economies of scale">economies of scale</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Rodrigue&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2013&#95;56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rodrigue_et_al_2013-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>56<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Automobiles provide high flexibility with low capacity, but require high energy and area use, and are the main source of harmful <a href="/wiki/Noise_pollution" title="Noise pollution">noise</a> and <a href="/wiki/Air_pollution" title="Air pollution">air pollution</a> in cities;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>57<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> buses allow for more efficient travel at the cost of reduced flexibility.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998279&#95;45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998279-45"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Road transport by truck is often the initial and <a href="/wiki/Last_mile_(transportation)" title="Last mile (transportation)">final stage</a> of freight transport.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Rodrigue&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2013&#95;56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rodrigue_et_al_2013-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>56<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Water">Water</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Water"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Maritime_transport" title="Maritime transport">Maritime transport</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jadrolinija_supetar_ferry.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Jadrolinija_supetar_ferry.JPG/250px-Jadrolinija_supetar_ferry.JPG" decoding="async" width="250" height="123" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Jadrolinija_supetar_ferry.JPG/500px-Jadrolinija_supetar_ferry.JPG 1.5x" data-file-width="2134" data-file-height="1049" /></a><figcaption>Automobile ferry in <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Water transport is movement by means of a <a href="/wiki/Watercraft" title="Watercraft">watercraft</a>—such as a <a href="/wiki/Barge" title="Barge">barge</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boat" title="Boat">boat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ship" title="Ship">ship</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Sailboat" title="Sailboat">sailboat</a>—over a body of water, such as a <a href="/wiki/Sea" title="Sea">sea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ocean" title="Ocean">ocean</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake" title="Lake">lake</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canal" title="Canal">canal</a>, or <a href="/wiki/River" title="River">river</a>. The need for buoyancy is common to watercraft,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>58<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> making the <a href="/wiki/Hull_(watercraft)" title="Hull (watercraft)">hull</a> a critical aspect of its construction, maintenance, and appearance.
</p><p>In the 19th century, the first <a href="/wiki/Steam_ships" class="mw-redirect" title="Steam ships">steam ships</a> were developed, using a <a href="/wiki/Steam_engine" title="Steam engine">steam engine</a> to drive a <a href="/wiki/Paddle_wheel" title="Paddle wheel">paddle wheel</a> or <a href="/wiki/Propeller" title="Propeller">propeller</a> to move the ship. The <a href="/wiki/Steam" title="Steam">steam</a> was produced in a <a href="/wiki/Boiler" title="Boiler">boiler</a> using wood or <a href="/wiki/Coal" title="Coal">coal</a> and fed through a steam <a href="/wiki/External_combustion_engine" title="External combustion engine">external combustion engine</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>59<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Now most commercial ships have an <a href="/wiki/Internal_combustion_engine" title="Internal combustion engine">internal combustion engine</a> using a slightly refined type of <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a> called <a href="/wiki/Bunker_fuel" class="mw-redirect" title="Bunker fuel">bunker fuel</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>60<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Some ships, such as <a href="/wiki/Submarine" title="Submarine">submarines</a>, use <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_marine_propulsion" title="Nuclear marine propulsion">nuclear marine propulsion</a> with heat from a <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_reactor" title="Nuclear reactor">nuclear reactor</a> generating the steam.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Recreation" title="Recreation">Recreational</a> or <a href="/wiki/Education" title="Education">educational</a> craft still use wind power or oars, while some smaller craft use <a href="/wiki/Internal_combustion_engines" class="mw-redirect" title="Internal combustion engines">internal combustion engines</a> to drive one or more <a href="/wiki/Propeller" title="Propeller">propellers</a> or, in the case of jet boats, an inboard water jet.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>62<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In shallow draft areas, <a href="/wiki/Hovercraft" title="Hovercraft">hovercraft</a> are propelled by large pusher-prop fans.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>63<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> (See <a href="/wiki/Marine_propulsion" title="Marine propulsion">Marine propulsion</a>.)
</p><p>Although it is slow compared to other transport, modern <a href="/wiki/Sea_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Sea transport">sea transport</a> is a highly efficient method of transporting large quantities of goods. <a href="/wiki/Commercial_vessel" class="mw-redirect" title="Commercial vessel">Commercial vessels</a>, nearly 35,000 in number, carried 7.4&#160;billion tons of cargo in 2007.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>64<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Transport by water is significantly less costly than air transport for transcontinental <a href="/wiki/Shipping" class="mw-redirect" title="Shipping">shipping</a>;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEStopford19974–6&#95;65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStopford19974–6-65"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>65<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Short_sea_shipping" class="mw-redirect" title="Short sea shipping">short sea shipping</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ferry" title="Ferry">ferries</a> remain viable in coastal areas.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEStopford19978–9&#95;66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStopford19978–9-66"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>66<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998280&#95;67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998280-67"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>67<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other_modes">Other modes</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Other modes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System_Luca_Galuzzi_2005.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Oil pipeline winding through cold Alaskan country-side. In the background are mountains, partly snow-capped" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System_Luca_Galuzzi_2005.jpg/250px-Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System_Luca_Galuzzi_2005.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System_Luca_Galuzzi_2005.jpg/500px-Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System_Luca_Galuzzi_2005.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1333" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Trans-Alaska_Pipeline" class="mw-redirect" title="Trans-Alaska Pipeline">Trans-Alaska Pipeline</a> for <a href="/wiki/Crude_oil" class="mw-redirect" title="Crude oil">crude oil</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pipeline_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Pipeline transport">Pipeline transport</a> sends goods through a <a href="/wiki/Pipe_(material)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pipe (material)">pipe</a>; most commonly, chemically stable liquids, vapors, and gases can be sent,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>68<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> while a <a href="/wiki/Slurry" title="Slurry">slurry</a> can be used to transport solids.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>69<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pneumatic_tube" title="Pneumatic tube">Pneumatic tubes</a> can send solid capsules using compressed air.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>70<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Short-distance systems exist for <a href="/wiki/Sewage" title="Sewage">sewage</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slurry" title="Slurry">slurry</a>, <a href="/wiki/Water" title="Water">water</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Beer" title="Beer">beer</a>, while long-distance networks are used for <a href="/wiki/Freshwater" class="mw-redirect" title="Freshwater">freshwater</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>71<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>72<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Natural_gas" title="Natural gas">natural gas</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>73<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Cable_transport" title="Cable transport">Cable transport</a> is a broad mode where vehicles are pulled by <a href="/wiki/Wire_rope" title="Wire rope">cables</a> instead of an internal power source. It is most commonly used at steep <a href="/wiki/Gradient" title="Gradient">gradient</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Neumann&#95;2010&#95;74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Neumann_2010-74"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>74<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Typical solutions include <a href="/wiki/Aerial_tramway" title="Aerial tramway">aerial tramways</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>75<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Funicular" title="Funicular">funiculars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elevator" title="Elevator">elevators</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>76<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Material_ropeway" title="Material ropeway">material ropeways</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>77<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Ski_lift" title="Ski lift">ski lifts</a>;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Neumann&#95;2010&#95;74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Neumann_2010-74"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>74<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> some of these are also categorized as <a href="/wiki/Conveyor_belt" title="Conveyor belt">conveyor</a> transport.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> A variant is the <a href="/wiki/Zip_line" title="Zip line">zip line</a>, which uses gravity for propulsion.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>78<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Space_transport" class="mw-redirect" title="Space transport">Spaceflight</a> is transport outside Earth's atmosphere by means of a <a href="/wiki/Spacecraft" title="Spacecraft">spacecraft</a>. It is most frequently used for <a href="/wiki/Satellite" title="Satellite">satellites</a> placed in Earth orbit.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>79<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> However, <a href="/wiki/Human_spaceflight" title="Human spaceflight">human spaceflight</a> mission have landed on the Moon<sup id="cite&#95;ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>80<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and are occasionally used to rotate crew-members to <a href="/wiki/Space_station" title="Space station">space stations</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>81<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Uncrewed_spacecraft" title="Uncrewed spacecraft">Uncrewed spacecraft</a> have been sent to all the planets of the Solar System.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>82<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Suborbital_spaceflight" class="mw-redirect" title="Suborbital spaceflight">Suborbital spaceflight</a> is the fastest of the existing and planned transport systems from a place on Earth to a distant "other place" on Earth.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>83<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These rocket-propelled systems could potentially do global point-to-point transport delivery of passengers or cargo in less than 90 minutes.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>84<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Elements">Elements</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Elements"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Infrastructure"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure" title="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GoldenGateBridge_BakerBeach_MC.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/GoldenGateBridge_BakerBeach_MC.jpg/250px-GoldenGateBridge_BakerBeach_MC.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/GoldenGateBridge_BakerBeach_MC.jpg/500px-GoldenGateBridge_BakerBeach_MC.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>Bridges, such as <a href="/wiki/Golden_Gate_Bridge" title="Golden Gate Bridge">Golden Gate Bridge</a>, allow roads and railways to cross bodies of water.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tampereen_rantatunneli.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Tampereen_rantatunneli.jpg/250px-Tampereen_rantatunneli.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Tampereen_rantatunneli.jpg/500px-Tampereen_rantatunneli.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="5077" data-file-height="3088" /></a><figcaption>Tunnels, such as the <a href="/wiki/Tampere_Tunnel" title="Tampere Tunnel">Tampere Tunnel</a>, allow traffic to pass underground or through rock formations.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Infrastructure is the fixed installations that allow a vehicle to operate. It consists of a transport network, a terminal, and facilities for parking and maintenance.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>85<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For rail, pipeline, road, and cable transport, the entire way the vehicle travels must be constructed. Air and watercraft are able to avoid this, since the <a href="/wiki/Airway_(aviation)" title="Airway (aviation)">airway</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sea_lane" title="Sea lane">seaway</a> do not need to be constructed. However, they require fixed infrastructure at terminals.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pollalis&#95;2016&#95;86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollalis_2016-86"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>86<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Terminals such as airports, ports, and stations, are locations where passengers and freight can be transferred from one vehicle or mode to another. For passenger transport, terminals are integrating different modes to allow riders, who are interchanging between modes, to take advantage of each mode's benefits.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Pollalis&#95;2016&#95;86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollalis_2016-86"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>86<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For instance, <a href="/wiki/Airport_rail_link" title="Airport rail link">airport rail links</a> connect airports to the city centres and suburbs. The terminals for automobiles are <a href="/wiki/Parking_lot" title="Parking lot">parking lots</a>, while buses and coaches can operate from simple stops.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998275–276&#95;87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECooperShepherd1998275–276-87"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>87<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For freight, terminals act as <a href="/wiki/Transshipment" title="Transshipment">transshipment</a> points,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>88<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> though some cargo is transported directly from the point of production to the point of use.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Transport_finance" title="Transport finance">financing</a> of infrastructure can either be <a href="/wiki/Government" title="Government">public</a> or <a href="/wiki/Private_enterprise" class="mw-redirect" title="Private enterprise">private</a>. Transport is often a <a href="/wiki/Natural_monopoly" title="Natural monopoly">natural monopoly</a><sup id="cite&#95;ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>89<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and a necessity for the public; roads, and in some countries railways and airports, are funded through <a href="/wiki/Taxation" class="mw-redirect" title="Taxation">taxation</a>. New infrastructure projects can have high costs and are often financed through <a href="/wiki/Debt" title="Debt">debt</a>. Many infrastructure owners, therefore, impose usage fees,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> such as <a href="/wiki/Landing_fees" class="mw-redirect" title="Landing fees">landing fees</a> at airports or <a href="/wiki/Toll_plaza" class="mw-redirect" title="Toll plaza">toll plazas</a> on roads.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>90<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Independent of this, authorities may impose <a href="/wiki/Tax" title="Tax">taxes</a> on the purchase or use of vehicles.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>91<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Because of poor forecasting and overestimation of passenger numbers by planners, there is frequently a benefits shortfall for transport infrastructure projects.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>92<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Means_of_transport">Means of transport</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Means of transport"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Animals">Animals</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Animals"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Animals used in transportation include <a href="/wiki/Pack_animals" class="mw-redirect" title="Pack animals">pack animals</a> and <a href="/wiki/Riding_animal" class="mw-redirect" title="Riding animal">riding animals</a>. These include various <a href="/wiki/Bovids" class="mw-redirect" title="Bovids">bovids</a>, <a href="/wiki/Equids" class="mw-redirect" title="Equids">equids</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Camelids" class="mw-redirect" title="Camelids">camelids</a>; animal families noted for their muscular strength.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>93<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Other species employed for various forms of transport include the dog, elephant, ostrich, sheep, and even the <a href="/wiki/Military_marine_mammal" title="Military marine mammal">dolphin</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Vehicles">Vehicles</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Vehicles"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Vehicle" title="Vehicle">Vehicle</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BMW_U06_IMG_6802.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/BMW_U06_IMG_6802.jpg/250px-BMW_U06_IMG_6802.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="142" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/BMW_U06_IMG_6802.jpg/500px-BMW_U06_IMG_6802.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="4768" data-file-height="2699" /></a><figcaption>A <a href="/wiki/BMW_2_Series_Active_Tourer" title="BMW 2 Series Active Tourer">BMW 2 Series Active Tourer</a> in 2022</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Customized_motorcycle_to_maximize_load_capacity.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Customized_motorcycle_to_maximize_load_capacity.jpg/250px-Customized_motorcycle_to_maximize_load_capacity.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="207" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Customized_motorcycle_to_maximize_load_capacity.jpg/500px-Customized_motorcycle_to_maximize_load_capacity.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2974" data-file-height="2457" /></a><figcaption>Customized motorcycle to maximize load capacity. Mobility is important for motorcycles, which are primarily used for transporting light cargo in urban areas.</figcaption></figure>
<p>A vehicle is a non-living device that is used to move people and goods. Unlike the infrastructure, the vehicle moves along with the cargo and riders. Unless being pulled/pushed by a cable or muscle-power, the vehicle must provide its own propulsion; this is most commonly done through a <a href="/wiki/Steam_engine" title="Steam engine">steam engine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Internal_combustion_engine" title="Internal combustion engine">combustion engine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Electric_motor" title="Electric motor">electric motor</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>94<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Jet_engine" title="Jet engine">jet engine</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Rocket" title="Rocket">rocket</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>95<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> though other means of propulsion also exist such as sail power or <a href="/wiki/Compressed-air_car" title="Compressed-air car">compressed air</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>96<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Vehicles also need a system of converting the energy into movement; this is most commonly done through <a href="/wiki/Wheel" title="Wheel">wheels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Propeller" title="Propeller">propellers</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Air_pressure" class="mw-redirect" title="Air pressure">air pressure</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>97<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Vehicles are commonly staffed by a <a href="/wiki/Driving" title="Driving">driver</a>. However, some systems, such as <a href="/wiki/People_mover" title="People mover">people movers</a> and some rapid transits, are fully <a href="/wiki/Automation" title="Automation">automated</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>98<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For <a href="/wiki/Passenger" title="Passenger">passenger</a> transport, the vehicle must have a compartment, seat, or platform for the passengers. Simple vehicles, such as automobiles, bicycles, or simple aircraft, may have one of the passengers as a driver. Since 2016, progress related to the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Industrial_Revolution" title="Fourth Industrial Revolution">Fourth Industrial Revolution</a> has brought a lot of new emerging technologies for transportation and automotive fields such as <a href="/wiki/Connected_car" title="Connected car">connected vehicles</a><sup id="cite&#95;ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>99<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Vehicular_automation" title="Vehicular automation">autonomous vehicles</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>100<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These innovations are said to form future mobility, but concerns remain on safety and cybersecurity, particularly concerning connected and autonomous mobility.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>101<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Operation">Operation</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Operation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Incheon_International_Airport.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Tilted aerial view of modern airport. Aircraft are parked next to &quot;arms&quot; that extend from the central building" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/7/76/Incheon_International_Airport.jpg/250px-Incheon_International_Airport.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/7/76/Incheon_International_Airport.jpg/500px-Incheon_International_Airport.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Incheon_International_Airport" title="Incheon International Airport">Incheon International Airport</a>, South Korea</figcaption></figure>
<p>Private transport is only subject to the owner of the vehicle, who operates the vehicle themselves. For public transport and freight transport, operations are done through <a href="/wiki/Private_enterprise" class="mw-redirect" title="Private enterprise">private enterprise</a>, <a href="/wiki/Government" title="Government">governments</a>, or a partnership between the two.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Mizutani&#95;2006&#95;102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mizutani_2006-102"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>102<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Kaminsky&#95;2018&#95;5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kaminsky_2018-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The infrastructure and vehicles may be owned and operated by the same company, or they may be operated by different entities. Traditionally, many countries have had a <a href="/wiki/Flag_carrier" title="Flag carrier">national airline</a> and <a href="/wiki/Railway_company" class="mw-redirect" title="Railway company">national railway</a>. Since the 1980s, many of these have been <a href="/wiki/Privatization" title="Privatization">privatized</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>103<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> International shipping remains a highly competitive industry with little regulation,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEStopford1997422&#95;104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStopford1997422-104"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>104<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but ports can be public-owned.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEStopford199729&#95;105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStopford199729-105"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>105<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Policy">Policy</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Policy"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_ministries_of_transport_by_country" class="mw-redirect" title="List of ministries of transport by country">List of ministries of transport by country</a> and <a href="/wiki/Traffic_management" title="Traffic management">Traffic management</a></div>
<p>As the <a href="/wiki/World_population" title="World population">population of the world</a> increases, <a href="/wiki/Urbanization" title="Urbanization">cities grow in size</a> and population—according to the United Nations, 55% of the world's population live in cities, and by 2050 this number is expected to rise to 68%.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>106<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Public_transport" title="Public transport">Public transport</a> policy must evolve to meet the changing priorities of the urban world.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>107<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The institution of policy enforces a degree of order in transport, which is by nature chaotic as people attempt to travel from one place to another as rapidly as possible.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>108<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> This policy helps to reduce accidents and save lives.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Functions">Functions</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Functions"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Relocation of travelers and cargo are the most common uses of transport. However, other uses exist, such as the transfer of mobile construction and emergency equipment, or the strategic and tactical <a href="/wiki/Military_logistics" title="Military logistics">relocation</a> of <a href="/wiki/Armed_force" class="mw-redirect" title="Armed force">armed forces</a> during <a href="/wiki/Warfare" class="mw-redirect" title="Warfare">warfare</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Passenger">Passenger<span class="anchor" id="Passenger"></span></h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transport&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Passenger"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Travel" title="Travel">Travel</a> and <a href="/wiki/Public_transit" class="mw-redirect" title="Public transit">Public transit</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Transperth_Bus_Number_2762.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Light green, orange, and white bus stopping in front of multi-story building." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Transperth_Bus_Number_2762.jpg/250px-Transperth_Bus_Number_2762.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Transperth_Bus_Number_2762.jpg/500px-Transperth_Bus_Number_2762.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2227" data-file-height="1485" /></a><figcaption>A local transit bus operated by <a href="/wiki/Transperth" title="Transperth">Transperth</a> in <a href="/wiki/Perth" title="Perth">Perth</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Passenger transport, or travel, is divided into <a href="/wiki/Public_transport" title="Public transport">public</a> and <a href="/wiki/Private_transport" title="Private transport">private transport</a>. Public transport is scheduled services on fixed routes, while private service can be scheduled (e.g. commercial airlines) or chartered (e.g. <a href="/wiki/Chartering_(shipping)" title="Chartering (shipping)">shipping</a>) or can provide ad hoc services at the riders desire (e.g. taxi).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Mizutani&#95;2006&#95;102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mizutani_2006-102"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>102<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The latter offers better flexibility, but has lower capacity and a higher environmental impact. Travel may be as part of daily <a href="/wiki/Commuting" title="Commuting">commuting</a> or for <a href="/wiki/Business_travel" title="Business travel">business</a>, leisure, or <a href="/wiki/Human_migration" title="Human migration">migration</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Hoogma&#95;et&#95;al&#95;2005&#95;109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hoogma_et_al_2005-109"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>109<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Short-haul transport is dominated by the automobile and mass transit.
